儒鴻(1476 TT) Trading Buy

營運穩定成長，長期展望正向。 
投資建議：預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，
稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027 年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂
單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，YoY+3.84%，稅 後 EPS 25.52
元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，預期未來幾個季
度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新提高
生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投
產，推動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為
Trading Buy，目標價 385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。  
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元：儒鴻 3Q26 營收
維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%， QoQ+4.82%，毛利率亦
受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因
素，順利提升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。預期
4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻
營收維持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，
YoY+14.34%，QoQ+0.20%，毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維
持六個月左右：美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期
極端的關稅政策不會再發生，不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目
前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，預期未來幾個月的
訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能見度維
持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因
應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產：儒鴻持續擴增新產能及
自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南二地的生
產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目
前印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來
的成長空間仍大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下
一段成長的動能。

### Page 2

個股報告

2 群益投顧 
  儒鴻的 ESG：  
儒鴻 2016 年即制定溫室氣體盤查管理程序，並依循「儒鴻企業環安衛及能
源政策」設置溫室氣體盤查推行委員會，由董事長室擔任主任委員，推動廠
區溫室氣體盤查，並以 2017 年作為公司溫室氣體盤查的基準年，推動溫室
氣體減量。此外，致力於用水回收，除了在製程蒸氣冷凝回收使用外，更引
進新技術改善廢水回收。 
公司簡介： 
儒鴻為台灣主要紡織業者，具染整、織布、成衣上下游一貫生產能力，主要
產品為各式成衣，並生產機能布料應用在各類運動休閒服飾上。國際各知名
服飾品牌及通路商如 NIKE、 UA、 LULULEMON 、 TARGET、 COSTCO 皆
為儒鴻的客戶。 
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元： 
儒鴻 3Q26 營收維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%，QoQ+4.82%，毛利率
亦受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因素，順利提
升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。 
預期 4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻營收維
持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，YoY+14.34%， QoQ+0.20%，
毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維持六個月左
右： 
美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期極端的關稅政策不會再發生，
不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，
預期未來幾個月的訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能
見度維持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，
印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產： 
儒鴻持續擴增新產能及自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南
二地的生產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目前
印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來的成長空間仍
大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下一段成長的動能。 
投資建議： 
預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027
年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，
YoY+3.84%，稅後 EPS 25.52 元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，
預期未來幾個季度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新
提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產，推
動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為 Trading Buy，目標價
385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。 
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

Advanced Energy Solution (6781.TW)
Growth Intact Despite A Longer HVDC Transition
CITI'S TAKE 
We walked away from AES’s 3Q briefing more confident in the BBU growth 
outlook. Resilient low-voltage demand and higher-power product 
upgrades should sustain growth through the HVDC transition, with 
400V/800V commercialization targeted for mid/end-2027E respectively. 
Rising technical complexity should favor AES’s engineering expertise and 
established customer relationships. We trim our TP to NT$1,550 (from 
NT$1,680) on 25x 2027E P/E from 27x reflecting HVDC timing uncertainty. 
Reiterate Buy as rising BBU penetration and content per rack underpin 
structural growth.
3Q26: strong BBU demand drives growth and mix upgrades lift GPM y/y — 3Q 
sales of NT$5.2bn rose 27% y/y and 9% q/q, driven by strong BBU demand and were 
9% above our forecast. GPM expanded 1.3ppt y/y to 36.3% on mix upgrades, though 
it fell 2.8ppt q/q. Op profits rose 34% y/y with OPM up 1.4ppt y/y to 24.6%. Net profit 
of NT$1.1bn was up 34% y/y, 3% above our forecast but 5% below consensus. Higher 
opex and lower non-op income partly offset the sales beat.
Low voltage BBUs retain a substantial growth runway — Mgmt expects steady 4Q 
growth and remains positive on 2027E, with no downward revisions to customer 
demand plans. Continued datacenter buildout should sustain low-voltage BBU 
procurement while HVDC architectures undergo qualifications. Demand for 3kW 
products remains broad across conventional servers and lower power datacenter 
workloads. AES is working with several new CSPs and overseas SIs, with new 
customer contributions expected in 2027E. Rising GPU power, greater BBU 
penetration and higher content per MW should support volumes and mix-led ASP 
growth. Cell prices are rising, but AES indicates stable procurement through long-
standing relationships with suppliers and will seek to pass on cost increases. 
HVDC progress: commercialization likely in 2H27E — High voltage BBU 
commercialization has been delayed to 2H27E, reflecting product development and 
system integration challenges. Currently samples have been delivered and large-
scale testing is underway for 400V products, with mass production targeted for 
mid-2027E and 800V products are scheduled for end-2027E. Mgmt. sees high 
voltage BBU ASP to be 5-10x higher than low voltage products. We believe 
increasing system complexity and qualification requirements favor AES’s 
established engineering capabilities and CSP relationships, though shipment 
timing and margin contribution require monitoring.

Non-BBU development: new applications broaden the growth base — Mgmt

Advanced Energy Solution (6781.TW)
09 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Advanced Energy Solution
Company description
Established in January 2020 and listed in March 2021, Advanced Energy
Solution Holdings (AES) is a holding company registered in Cayman Island,
supplying battery packs for LEV (light electrical vehicle such as e-Bikes) and
BBU (battery back-up unit for datacenters) applications. Prior to the
establishment and IPO, AES was a BU focusing on non-IT applications within

its parent company, Simplo Technology (6121.TW).
Investment strategy
We rate AES shares as Buy. Being an industry leader in battery pack design
and manufacturing with a proven track record, we expect AES to benefit from
higher BBU content per rack and deep CSP engagement as the industry
transitions from conventional 48V systems to HVDC architecture. BBU now
contributes >70% of sales and should remain the key earnings driver as AI
rack power continues to rise. We forecast earnings CAGR of 27% in 2025-

2028E, supported by strong BBU demand, mix upgrade toward high-power
BBU products and op leverage.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$1,550 is based on 25x 2027E EPS on par with its
historical average since listing and supported by a 2025-28E earnings CAGR
of 27%. We use 2027E as the year of valuation base as we think 2027E can
better reflect its earnings power after the commercializarion of HVDC
products.
Risks
Key downside risks to our earnings forecasts that could prevent the shares

from reaching our target price include: 1) slower-than-expected end demand
from e-Bikes post the pandemic and volaitlity in cloud capex deployment that
could impact datacenter BBU sales; 2) component supply
constraints/logistics challenges, which could lead to slower-than-expected
shipment and top-line momentum; and 3) peer competition, which could
impact sales growth and margins negatively, in our view.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +14% MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$74,666mn (+14% MoM/+30% YoY), driven by
the increasing shipment of power supply units, HVDC power racks, and liquid
cooling products.
• QTD revenue reached 35% of MSe at NT$215,881mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY),
and 34% of consensus at NT$219,806mn (+12% QoQ/+36% YoY).

Our view:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We think the slightly better Oct monthly sales should help on near-term
sentiment, though more meaningful upside to market expectations on
earnings growth might  depend on the penetration of HVDC power racks into
the coming years.
• We continue to see Delta as a key beneficiary of the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters through its comprehensive power/cooling

offerings. Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(YoY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(YoY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(YoY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(YoY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(YoY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(YoY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

Document 260: AI资本开支见顶=美元见顶？高盛2027展望：美元即将迎来“最后一涨”

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI资本开支见顶=美元见顶？高盛2027展望：美元即将迎来“最后一涨”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛认为，美元短期内仍有上⾏空间，但 2027 年将迎来实质性逆转。⽀撑这轮涨势的核⼼
驱动⼒正是 AI 相关投资预期的持续升温，⽽⼀旦这⼀预期⻅顶，美元也将⾯临同样的拐
点。
摘要：
美元与 AI 资本开⽀预期深度绑定，⾼盛警告：当 AI 叙事退潮，美元将随之承压。
⾼盛外汇策略团队在《 2027 全球外汇展望》报告中指出，美元短期内仍有上⾏空间，但 2027 年
将迎来实质性逆转。在历经 2025 年的⼤规模抛售与多元化配置后，美元于 2026 年悄然收复失地
—— 欧元兑美元回落⾄ " 解放⽇ " 以来低位，美元指数（ DXY ）触及 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最⾼点，美元
兑⽇元逼近 160 关⼝。⾼盛认为，⽀撑这轮涨势的核⼼驱动⼒正是 AI 相关投资预期的持续升温，
⽽⼀旦这⼀预期⻅顶，美元也将⾯临同样的拐点。
数据显⽰，外国投资者在截⾄今年 8 ⽉的⼀年间净购⼊创纪录的 1.47 万亿美元美国股票及企业债
券，⼏乎是全球⾦融危机前峰值的两倍。外资持有的美国股票中，约 90% 为账⾯浮盈⽽⾮实际

增量买⼊，⽽对冲⽐率处于历史低位 —— 换⾔之，仅对冲⽐率的结构性上移，就⾜以在⽆需⼤
规模资本外流的情况下对美元形成实质性压制。⾼盛警告， AI 资本开⽀预期⼀旦显现⻅顶迹
象，投资者可能要求更⼤的价格折让 —— 包括汇率层⾯的让步。
美元重回⾼估区间， AI 成为核⼼⽀撑变量
美元估值已重回历史偏⾼⽔平。⾼盛汇率错配模型显⽰，贸易加权美元较合理价值⾼估约
13% ，较 9 ⽉时的 7% ⼤幅上升；对亚洲货币的⾼估幅度则始终维持在 25% 左右，从未真正收
窄。
⾼盛策略师 Kamakshya Trivedi 与 Michael Cahill 在报告中明确将美元定性为 "AI 交易 " ，" 美元表
现与 AI 相关股票表现⾼度挂钩 —— ⽆论⾏情好坏皆然。 " 这⼀判断背后有数据⽀撑：在外国资本
流⼊美国的结构中，私⼈购买美国企业股票的⽐例已飙升⾄ 40 年历史区间的顶端。
值得注意的是， 2026 年外汇市场呈现出⼀个罕⻅悖论：全年外汇持仓变动幅度创历史之最，但
实际汇率波动区间却极为狭窄。⾼盛将这⼀现象归结为低衰退⻛险与有限政策分化并存的市场

环境，使得套息交易主导全年，⽅向性押注则普遍受挫。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

美联储鹰派⽴场为美元提供短期⽀撑
⾼盛并不建议当下即做空美元，原因在于美联储持续偏鹰的政策信号。在最新通胀数据显著超
预期 ——70 位预测⼈⼠中仅⼀位预测值更⾼ —— 后，⾼盛经济学家将今年加息次数上调⾄三
次。
10 年期美债收益率已重返 5% 上⽅，最新 PPI 数据创四年新⾼，均构成对美元的阶段性⽀撑。⾼
盛基准预测认为，通胀最终将趋于温和，美联储实际收紧幅度将远⼩于市场当前定价，届时美
元将承压。但该⾏坦承，近期⻛险仍 " 偏向美元⾛强 " ，尤其若美国⾦融条件收紧的外溢效应进⼀
步暴露欧洲脆弱性。
就欧元⽽⾔，⾼盛预测欧元兑美元将在三个⽉内跌⾄ 1.08 ，主因能源冲击带来的贸易条件恶化效
应滞后传导，以及法国上半年经济接近停滞（该预测低于市场普遍预期）；随后于 2027 年底反弹
⾄ 1.15 。⾼盛同时指出，法国的可信财政整固措施可能要等到 6 ⽉议会选举之后才能落地，⽽此
类措施往往需要市场压⼒作为政治推⼿。
⽇元：低估 30% ，政策转向提供结构性⽀撑
如果说美元是 AI 交易的多头，⽇元则是⾼盛设定的 AI 对冲⼯具。按照该⾏⾸选的公允价值模型，

⽇元对美元低估幅度约达 30% ，是 G10 货币中估值偏差最为显著的货币之⼀。
政策层⾯，⽇本央⾏的⽴场正在切实转变。⾼盛经济学家预计，⽇本央⾏将于 2027 年 6 ⽉前将政
策利率升⾄ 2% ，意味着还有三次 25 个基点的加息，⾼于市场⽬前约 1.75% 的隐含定价。该⾏还
注意到，⾃ 10 ⽉初以来，在纳斯达克跑输标普 500 指数逾 1% 的交易⽇内，⽇元平均涨幅约为
0.3% ，是 G10 表现最佳的货币。
⾼盛对美元兑⽇元的路径预测为：三个⽉ 158 、六个⽉ 155 、⼗⼆个⽉ 150 ，较当前约 160 的⽔平
下跌约 6% 。该⾏同时警⽰， " 若市场对 AI 预期的质疑更为显著，调整幅度可能⼤幅超出上述预
测 " 。
AI 债务泡沫与对冲⽐率是两⼤隐患
AI 叙事的脆弱性不仅体现在股票估值层⾯。
全球 AI 相关债务发⾏规模在 11 ⽉初已达 6870 亿美元，⽽超额流动性正在收缩，盈利上调动能也
在放缓。⾼盛估算， AI 投资约占美国实际 GDP 三季度同⽐增⻓的三分之⼆，⼀旦融资环境收

紧，负债最重的参与者将⾸当其冲，潜在冲击将远超科技⾏业范畴。
德意志银⾏策略师 George Saravelos 此前亦发出类似警告，指出外国持有的逾 20 万亿美元美国
股票中，约 90% 为账⾯价格升值⽽⾮实际买⼊，且整体对冲⽐率极低 —— 换⾔之，仅对冲⽐率
的结构性上移，就⾜以在⽆需⼤规模资本外流的情况下对美元形成实质性压制。
2027 年汇市格局：谁赢谁输
⾼盛 2027 年外汇总回报排名围绕四⼤主线展开：商品敏感货币随能源价格正常化⽽反弹、 AI 溢
出效应持续向亚洲货币传导、套息收益贡献收窄但依然为正、以及⽇元获得可持续政策⽀撑。
在预期总回报排名中，⾼息货币巴西雷亚尔（约 15% ）与南⾮兰特（约 10% ）位居前列；以⾊
列谢克尔、挪威克朗以及美元本⾝则处于末位。

Document 321: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-15).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The monthly household spending indicator rose 0.6%mth in
September (nominal), exceeding both Westpac and market
expectations. We had anticipated a 0.5%mth contraction
(on the basis of a softer read on vehicle sales in the month),

while the market had pencilled in a 0.2%mth rise. Today’s
result instead marks a return to growth following August’s
brief contraction (revised down 0.1ppt to –0.1%mth), with
annual growth strengthening to 6.8%yr. For Q3 as a whole,
nominal spending rose 2.1%qtr, the strongest quarterly
result since the December quarter of 2022. In real terms,
volumes increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, pointing to a
sharp acceleration in price growth through the quarter.

Non-discretionary spending outperforms
By broad category, services spending more than reversed
last month’s decline, rising a solid 1.1%mth (+6.8%yr). Goods
spending held around its recent pace, increasing 0.3%mth
(+6.7%yr). Discretionary spending returned to growth in
September, rising 0.3%mth (+6.9%yr), although this failed to
fully unwind August’s decline.
The relative strength in non-discretionary spending observed
last month continued into September, with the category rising

1.2%mth (+6.5%yr). While the headline result was stronger,
the breakdown aligns with findings from our Westpac–DataX
Card Tracker, and Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel which
show discretionary goods continuing to underperform other
categories (see here and here).
At the detailed level, transport and health led the gains,
both rising 1.5%mth. The increase in transport spending was
largely attributable to higher fuel spending, up 5.4%mth,
reflecting elevated fuel prices, with volumes down 2.2%mth.

The ABS also noted higher spending on vehicle repairs
and maintenance, while domestic air travel increased as
households travelled to attend major sporting events.
Recreation & culture rose 0.8%mth, while miscellaneous
goods & services (+0.6%mth), hotels, cafes & restaurants
(+0.5%mth), and clothing & footwear (+0.1%mth) also
recorded gains.
Partially offsetting these increases, food spending declined
0.1%mth, while furnishings & household equipment fell

0.2%mth, likely reflecting the ongoing weakness in housing
turnover (see here). Alcoholic beverages & tobacco recorded
the largest decline, falling 0.7%mth.
State outcomes were positive across the board in September.
WA led the gains, rising 1.1%mth and reversing last
month’s decline. Victoria also rebounded (+1.0%mth),
while Queensland (+0.6%mth), SA (+0.6%mth), Tasmania
(+0.5%mth) and NSW (+0.3%mth) all recorded increases.

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Volume growth remains firm 
Nominal spending rose a firm 2.1%qtr in Q3, while volumes 
increased a more subdued 0.7%qtr, matching the pace recorded 
in Q2. This implies a sharp acceleration in price growth to 
1.4%qtr, from 0.6%qtr in Q2, and marks the strongest quarterly 
increase in prices since the December quarter of 2022. Through 
the year, volume growth strengthened to 3.2%yr, remaining 
well above the 1.8%yr average recorded through 2025.
Transport led gains in volume terms, rising 1.6%qtr, 
likely partly reflecting base effects in air travel following 
disruptions associated with the Middle East conflict in the June 
quarter. Clothing & footwear (+1.2%qtr), health (+1.1%qtr), 
miscellaneous goods & services (+1.1%qtr), food (+0.5%qtr), 
recreation & culture (+0.5%qtr), and furnishings & household 
equipment (+0.1%qtr) also recorded increases. Hotels, cafes 
& restaurants spending declined 0.1%qtr in volume terms, 
while alcoholic beverages & tobacco was the main drag, falling 
1.2%qtr.
Most states recorded real spending growth over the quarter, 
led by WA (+1.4%qtr), Victoria (+0.9%qtr), NSW (+0.5%qtr), 
Queensland (+0.5%qtr) and SA (+0.4%qtr). Tasmania lagged its 
state peers, recording a 0.1%qtr decline.
Conclusion
The household spending indicator provides a useful guide to 
around two-thirds of total consumer expenditure. Importantly, 
it excludes several major categories included in the National 
Accounts measure of real household consumption, including 
rents and utilities.

Today’s volumes outcome was broadly in line with our 
expectations, with Westpac anticipating around a 0.6%qtr rise 
in Q3, and remains consistent with our view on consumption 
ahead of the Q3 National Accounts (due 2 December). 
However, this will likely be the high water mark for spending. 
The renewed deterioration in global energy markets and the 
cumulative effect of four rate increases over the first three 
quarters of the year are likely to weigh more heaveily on 
household spending in Q4.

Looking further ahead, households will continue to face 
a restrictive monetary policy environment, with the RBA 
delivering an additional rate increase at yesterday’s November 
meeting (see here). Combined with persistent consumer 
pessimism, ongoing cost-of-living pressures (see here) and a 
housing downturn impacting household wealth, spending looks 
set to slow further in 2027.

Document 354: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-15).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• As polls suggested, the election was a nailbiter
that has not yet been resolved. Election night
results show a lead to the centre-right on
paper in a significantly enlarged parliament.
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First
and ACT won 67 seats. The centre-left bloc
of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti Māori won 57
seats. There was a swing away from National
to its coalition partners while the centre left
grouping picked up a few seats.

• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling
and will enter parliament with at least 7 seats,
reducing what could have been a larger swing
to the centre-left grouping.
• The size of parliament will expand by around 9
seats as National held onto a large share of its
electorate votes, entitling it to more seats than
implied by its party vote share. As a result,
a bloc would require 66 seats to command a
majority in parliament.
• Several senior National ministers have not

been re-elected, including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. She will continue in a caretaker
role but will need to be replaced in any new
centre-right government.
• Significant uncertainty about the result exists.
National might lose perhaps two marginal
electorates in the final analysis. There are
400,000 special votes to count which may
shift the list seat allocations as these tend
to reduce the share of the centre-right bloc
by 1.6-1.7ppts. Hence, when all is said and

done, the centre-right’s 10 seat advantage on
election night could reduce by 1-4 seats.
• Should this play out the centre-right would not
have sufficient seats to reach a majority in an
enlarged house and would require the support
of the Opportunity Party to govern.
• The final election outcome will not be
known until 27 November. Serious coalition
negotiations likely won’t begin until then
and delays could occur. Should recounts be
required, some further delay is possible.

• Based on these results, we see a higher
probability of a centre-right government
ultimately emerging that likely will require
the support of the Opportunity Party in some
form. The Opportunity Party likely won’t enter
Cabinet but may offer support on confidence
and supply in exchange for policy concessions.
• But a centre-left government can’t be ruled
out if special votes heavily favour the centre
left bloc, and especially if an agreement
between the Opportunity Party and the centre-

right parties can’t be reached.
• Regardless of the final count, the results
suggest a government with weak support and
ongoing vulnerabilities. Hence the prospect of
controversial large changes may be modest.
For a centre-right government that might
limit the extent of fiscal consolidation, while
the centre left’s plans for larger scale tax and
spending initiatives could be trimmed back.
• A centre-right government won’t change the
RBNZ mandate while a centre-left government

likely still will. A new finance minister will be
required with the possibility of ACT leader
David Seymour taking on that role.
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
New Zealand 2026 General Election:
Preliminary results and next steps.
9 Nov 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz
Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
The result.
The preliminary results of the 2026 General Election,
based on the election night count, are set out in the table
above. In summary:
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First and ACT
won 67 seats.
• The centre-left bloc of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti
Māori won 57 seats.
• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling and will
enter parliament with 7 seats.
These results indicate a swing away from National to its

coalition partners while the centre-left grouping picked
up a few seats. The big gainer was the Opportunity Party
who perhaps took a big share of what otherwise could
have been a larger swing to the centre-left grouping.
The size of parliament will expand by around nine seats,
as National held onto a large share of its electorate
votes and hence has more seats than it would have been
entitled to based on its party vote share. As a result, a
governing bloc would require 66 seats to command a

majority in parliament.
Several of the electorate seats are very tight (see Table
2), so election night majorities could easily be overturned
once special votes are counted. Of particular relevance
is the seat of Wairarapa, where the National candidate is
leading the Labour candidate by just 139 votes. If Labour
were to win that seat (specials favoured Labour in 2020
but National in 2023), all else equal the centre-right seat
count would decline to 66 seats in what would become

a 130-seat Parliament – just enough to command a
majority. But if either NZ First or ACT were to lose a single
party vote seat to the left bloc once specials are counted
then that majority would disappear.
On that score, historically the election night results have
tended to overstate the support of the centre-right bloc
and understate the support for parties on the left of
the political spectrum. For example, over the previous
three elections (2017, 2020, 2023), the current centre-

right bloc’s vote share has declined 1.9%, 1.7% and 1.6%
respectively following the counting of special votes, with
the National Party losing 2 seats on each occasion once
the official results were declared (Labour, the Greens and
Te Pāti Māori have benefited). On this occasion, given its
overhang, a lower party vote share would not lower the
National Party’s final vote count, although it could alter
the size of its overhang and thus the number of seats

required to command a majority. In recent elections both
NZ First and ACT have seen their vote shares decline
following the counting of special votes.
The process from here.
Official results.
The official results will be declared on 27 November,
adding the results of an estimated 400,000 special votes
(with around a quarter of these cast overseas) to the
2.41m votes counted on election night.
We expect discussions between potential coalition
partners will begin immediately to start sketching out the

parameters of a coalition agreement. But given the tight
margins of the current vote count, no firm positions will
be agreed until the results are declared.
The writ and recounts.
After 27 November, the writ is scheduled to be returned
on 3 December – formally certifying who has been
elected. However, that date is subject to any recounts

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
that might be requested. Parliament is required to meet
no later than 14 January (six weeks after the return of the
writ), providing an opportunity for a new government
to test that it has the confidence of the House. It is
worth noting that Parliament can be summoned and
begin functioning even if coalition negotiations are still
occurring. If no coalition or confidence-and-supply
arrangement has been reached by then, negotiations can

simply continue and the incumbent Government remains
in office under the caretaker convention (Ministers in
the outgoing government retain executive authority
temporarily, even if they are no longer MPs).
Given that the Pre-Election Economic and Fiscal
Update was released in late September there is no legal
requirement for the Treasury to publish a Half Year
Economic and Fiscal Update (HYEFU) this year. And given
that it will be some weeks before a government is formed,

it seems unlikely that there will be a government in place
that would request that a HYEFU be produced despite
there being no statutory requirement (certainly there
would be little time to incorporate policy changes that
fall out of new coalition agreements, thus diminishing
its value). Once a government is formed, it will have an
opportunity to lay out its immediate priorities in the
Budget Policy Statement (BPS), which by law needs to
be released before the end of March next year. The new

government could decide to release an interim fiscal
update with the BPS if that was thought necessary.
Potential coalition outcomes.
We see three main likely outcomes:
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First.
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First with the support of the Opportunity Party
on confidence and supply.
• Some coalition of Labour, the Greens, Opportunity and
Te Pāti Māori, potentially with Opportunity and Te Pāti

Māori sitting outside the cabinet.
Our preview of the election discussed the policies of
the main political parties, while the figure on page 5
highlights some points of commonality with the centre-
right and centre-left blocs.
National, ACT and NZ First share a broadly pro-
development and deregulatory agenda, including support
for greater resource development, fewer regulatory
barriers and compulsory or expanded KiwiSaver. Beyond
that common core, National and ACT align particularly

closely on lower taxes and deregulation, while National
and NZ First share an emphasis on infrastructure, regional
development and law and order. ACT and NZ First favour
tighter immigration settings and tighter welfare policies.
The differences are clearest at the edges: ACT advocates
deeper tax cuts, smaller government and more market-
led provision, while NZ First places greater emphasis
on economic sovereignty, regional development and
targeted support for working New Zealanders. National

occupies more of the middle ground, combining fiscal
discipline and a pro-business agenda with increased
infrastructure and housing supply.
The Opportunity Party presents itself as a middle
alternative that can work with the left or the right
groupings. They have five bottom line policies they say
they need to support any new government. These include
competition reform through enhanced Commerce
Commission powers, more restrictions on political
lobbyists and donors, a ten-year plan of investment in the

healthcare sector, an end to bottom trawling in inshore
areas and a phase out elsewhere and for the Government
to recommit to New Zealand’s Paris Agreement
obligations. Their success in meeting these bottom
lines with a new government and their flexibility around
these requirements will be key to their ability to form an
agreement with either side. There is better congruence
of the Opportunity Party’s policies with the left than the
right – so progress here will be critical to watch should

their support be required to form a new government. We
suspect they will be very motivated to avoid the need for
a new election via a hung parliament should push come
to shove.

Labour, the Greens and Opportunity share support
for increased public and social investment, a broader

and stronger climate and environmental policy, while
Labour and Opportunity share an emphasis on social

investment and higher public-service wages. The Greens
and Opportunity both favour broader taxation and
stronger climate action, although their preferred tax
instruments differ. Their distinctive priorities are also
clear: Labour places more weight on worker protections
and restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate; the Greens on

Tiriti; and Opportunity on land-value taxation, housing

Document 360: Open mouth operations (2026-11-15)

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

Document 369: RBNZの予想外の利上げ見送りで、NZドル相場は一段と弱含むか ～追加利上げの時期や総選挙を巡る不透明感が重なり、豪ドルとの対照_7d032c38

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 28 日

1/4 
RBNZ の予想外の利上げ見送りで、NZ ドル相場は一段と弱含むか 
～追加利上げの時期や総選挙を巡る不透明感が重なり、豪ドルとの対照的な動きが続くか～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は、10 月 27～28 日に開催した定例理事会において、政
策金利を2.75％に据え置くことを決定した。RBNZ は７月に約３年ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、
９月にも追加利上げを実施するなど金融引き締めを強めてきた。このため、今回の据え置き
は３会合ぶりであり、金融市場の事前予想に反する決定となった。 
○ ７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と前期から鈍化したものの、コアインフレ率は加速す
るなど、インフレ圧力は依然として根強いことが確認された。このため、金融市場において
は RBNZ が今回利上げに動くことを織り込む動きが広がりをみせていた。こうしたなか、今回
の決定は４対３の僅差となったほか、声明文や総裁会見でも追加利上げの必要性が示された
ものの、そのタイミングについては不透明感が残る。 
○ また、11 月７日の総選挙を控え、RBNZ の政策目標の見直しが争点となっていることも、先行
きの政策運営を巡る不透明感につながっている。金融市場では、予想外の金利据え置きに対
する「失望」に加え、金融政策が後手に回るとの警戒感もＮＺドル相場の重しとなっている。
当面はＮＺドル相場の上値が重い展開が続くと見込まれ、オセアニア通貨のなかでも豪ドル
とＮＺドルが対照的な動きをみせる可能性がある。

【RBNZは事前予想に反して、10 月の定例会合で３会合ぶりに政策金利を据え置く決定】 
ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は、10 月 27～28 日に開催した定例理事会において、政策金
利であるオフィシャル・キャッシュ・レート（ＯＣＲ）を2.75％に据え置くことを決定した。ニュ
ージーランドでは、2025 年以降にインフレ率が加速し、RBNZ の定める目標レンジ（１～３％）の
上限を上回る伸びで推移している。さらに、イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油価格の上昇がエネルギ
ーインフレを招いたことから、RBNZ は７月の定例会合で約３年ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、９月会合
でも追加利上げに動くなど、金融引き締めを強化してきた（注１）。 このため、RBNZ による金利据

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html）

### Page 2

2/4 
え置きは３会合ぶりとなる。 
RBNZ のブレマン総裁は、９月の定例会合後の記者会見において、追加利上げに含みを持たせる考
えを示していた。一方で、予め決められた方針に固執せず、追加利上げのタイミングは極めて不確
実と述べるとともに、これまでの利上げの効果などを見極める考えをみせた。声明文でも、２会合
連続の利上げ実施を受けて、先行きは大幅利上げに動く必要性は低下したとの認識を示すなど、「 タ
カ派」姿勢が後退している様子がうかがえた。こうした姿勢の変化もあり、ＲＢＮＺによる断続的
な利上げ実施にもかかわらず、その後のＮＺドル相場は調整の動きを強めた。 
同国では、11 月７日に議会（代議院：一院制）の総選挙が実施されるが、RBNZ のマンデート（政
策目標）が争点のひとつとなっていることも、ＮＺドル相場が調整の動きを強める一因となった。
最大野党の左派・労働党を中心とする政
党連合は、マンデートに持続可能な雇用
の最大化を再び加えることを公約に掲げ
ている。世論調査では、右派の国民党を
中心とする与党連合、左派連合の競り合
いが続いており、新興政党・オポチュニ
ティ党の動向が注目されている（注２）。
足元の雇用環境をみると、直近４-６月の
失業率が 10 年ぶりの高水準となってお
り（図１）、仮にマンデートが変更されれ
ば、利上げのハードルが高まる可能性が
ある。こうした事情もＮＺドル相場の重しになったと考えられる。 
一方で、RBNZ のブレマン総裁は、９月の定例会合後の記者会見において、選挙結果が政策運営に
影響を与えることはないとの見方を示し
た。このため、金融市場では今回の定例
会合を占ううえで、物価動向に注目した。
７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と
５四半期連続で目標レンジを上回ったも
のの、前期（同＋4.1％）から鈍化し、RBNZ
が９月の定例会合で示した見通し（前年
比＋3.9％）も下回った（図２）。コアイ
ンフレ率は前年比＋2.9％と目標レンジ
内で推移しているものの、前期（同＋
2.6％）から加速し、７四半期ぶりの高い

### Page 3

3/4 
伸びとなるなど、インフレ圧力が高まっている様子が確認された。このため、金融市場では RBNZ が
追加利上げに動くとの見方が広がったものの、予想に反して政策金利を据え置いた格好となった。

【僅差での据え置き決定、市場の「失望」を反映してNZドル相場は上値の重い展開が続くか】 
会合後に公表された声明文では、今回の決定について「４（据え置き）対３（25bp の利上げ）の
賛成多数で金利据え置きを決定した」とした。足元の物価動向について「想定を下回った」、「賃金
上昇が抑制されており、中期的なインフレ期待は安定している」とする一方、「コアインフレ率は目
標レンジの上限近くに加速しており、物価上昇の動きが広範に及びつつある」との見方を示した。
先行きについては「世界的な情勢が短期的にインフレ圧力を一段と強めている」として、その要因
に「 原油高によるエネルギー価格の上昇と通貨ＮＺドル安」を挙げた。そして、「10-12 月のインフ
レ率は＋4.4％に加速が見込まれる」としつつ、「原油や石油製品価格の変動が激しく、インフレ見
通しは不透明」との見方を示した。 
実体経済については「回復が続いているものの、状況には依然ばらつきがある」との見方を示し
た。具体的には「外需関連産業では、商品市況の上昇、世界的な需要の堅調さ、外国人観光客数の
増加の恩恵を受けている」一方、「内需関連産業は低調に推移している」とした。一方で、「オーク
ランドを中心に住宅投資の回復の兆しがでている」とした。 
政策運営については「物価に対する一時的な要因を差し引いた上で、経済に不必要な不安定を起
こすことを回避しつつ、中期的にインフレ率を目標レンジの中央（２％）に戻すことに注力してい
る」とあらためて強調した。そのうえで、「家計消費が弱く、一部の企業ではコスト上昇の転嫁が制
限されている」としつつ、「インフレ率が高止まりすれば、コスト上昇がインフレ期待や価格や賃金
に波及してインフレがより持続的なものとなるリスクが高まる」との見方を示した。 
世界経済については「堅調に推移しているものの、地政学リスクやエネルギーインフレ、金融市
場のボラティリティの高まり、ＡＩ（人工知能）関連投資の急増などを背景に、その脆弱性は高ま
っている」との見方を示した。一方で、同国経済について「回復の動きが予想以上に力強くなる可
能性はある一方、世界経済の悪化、外需の好調さが雇用や所得、家計消費に波及しなければ勢いを
失う可能性がある」との認識を示した。そのうえで、今回の決定について「政策委員は全体的なイ
ンフレ見通しでは一致したが、個々のリスクに対する見方や金融政策の対応時期に関する意見は割
れた」、 先行きについても「追加利上げが必要となる可能性は高いとの見方で一致したものの、その
タイミングは経済見通し次第」として、追加利上げに含みを持たせる考えを示した。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだブレマン総裁は、今回の協議について「景気動向を勘案すれば、より
抑制的な政策が必要になることで合意した」、「ＮＺドル安が続けば輸入インフレを助長する可能性
がある」との認識が共有されたとした。先行きについては「さらなる引き締めが必要となり、状況
によっては引き締め的な領域に入る可能性がある」、「今後の会合で追加利上げを行う可能性が高い」
との認識を示した。そして、「ＯＣＲの道筋が９月会合の想定を上回る可能性についても協議した」
としつつ、「中立金利の見通しには幅があり、常に不確実性がある」として、具体的な水準への言及
は避けた。一方で、今回の決定については「経済見通しに基づいた決定である」としたうえで、「イ

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
ンフレ率を目標レンジに戻すための条件は依然として良好」、「賃金上昇率は目標とするインフレ動
向に見合う水準を下回っている」との認識を示した。 
前述したように、金融市場においては今回の利上げを織り込む動きが広がっていたため、予想外
の金利据え置き決定に対しては『失望』
にも似た反応がみられ、ＮＺドル相場は
調整の動きを強めている（図３）。背景に
は、総選挙の行方が不透明であることに
加え、その後の 12 月に開催される次回
の定例会合において、追加利上げが実施
されるか否かについても不透明感が残
ることがある。一部では、金融政策が後
手に回る（behind the curve）との警戒
感が強まっていることも影響している
と考えられる。このため、当面のＮＺド
ル相場は弱含む展開が続くと予想され、オセアニア通貨では豪ドルが強含む一方、ＮＺドルは上値
の重い展開が続くなど、両通貨が対照的な動きをみせる可能性が高まっている。

### Page 2

The Week Ahead November 6, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Taiwan

Export growth is expected to ease to 
44.4% yoy in October from 60.9% in 
September. A higher comparison base 
makes exceptional export growth 
difficult to sustain from 4Q onwards. The 
global AI cycle is also expected to settle 
into a more normal pace into 2027, as 
higher interest rates lift capital costs 
and US mid-term election results 
heighten regulatory uncertainty. These 
factors underpin our forecast for 
Taiwan’s GDP growth to return to high 
single digits from 4Q onwards and into 
2027.

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s industrial 
production growth to rise to 5.5% yoy in 
September 2026 from 5.0% yoy in 
August. The uptick was likely driven by 
export-oriented manufacturing as 
strong artificial intelligence (AI)-related 
demand continued to support growth in 
electrical and electronics goods, 
alongside a smaller contraction in mining.

China

Export growth is expected to decelerate 
from 36.3% yoy in September to 28.1% in 
October. Both the official manufacturing 
PMI and new export orders sub-index fell 
below 50 after two months in expansion 
territory. High-frequency trade indicators 
also point to slowing momentum, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage handled 
at China’s 20 major ports falling from 7.2% 
yoy in September to -4.2% in October. 
Freight activity weakened as well, with 
growth in international cargo flights 
dropping from 3.6% yoy to -5.9% over the 
same period. On the price front, factory-gate 
and raw material price pressures moderated, 
with the main raw material purchasing price 
and producer price sub-indices declining 
from 60.8 and 54.0 to 56.8 and 51.7, 
respectively. Despite remaining above the 
50 threshold, the moderation suggests some 
easing in pipeline price pressures. 
Downstream consumer price pressures are 
also likely to remain subdued amid soft 
domestic demand. As a result, CPI inflation 
is expected to ease slightly from 0.9% yoy in 
September to 0.8% in October.

India

We expect headline inflation to rise to 6.0% 
yoy in October from 5.5% the month before, 
on higher food costs and a low base effect. 
High frequency data pointed to a further 
pick-up in sequential pressures in food 
segments, across cereals, edible oil, 
perishables (vegetables) etc. Non-food 
components should find relief from further 
moderation in precious metals and delay in 
passage of retail fuel price changes. Core 
inflation is nonetheless set to quicken to 4.5-
4.6% as services notch increases and as 
indirect tax effects in the base, fall out of the 
equation. With inflation expected to test 
past the higher end of the RBI target range, 
we expect a second successive rate hike in 
December. The decline in oil prices has also 
led to cooling of overnight OIS rates as 
concerns over aggressive rate hikes have 
eased. Goods trade deficit is likely to stay 
wide at above $30bn in Oct.

Document 415: Westpac NZ Retail Spending Pulse, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Westpac NZ Retail Spending Pulse, October 2026 (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 7

### Page 1

RETAIL
SPENDING
PULSE
6 November 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Discretionary spending flat as high fuel
prices bite
• Per-person spending on Westpac issued credit and debit cards was up 1.2% in October and is 7.8%
higher than this time last year.
October spending.
• October’s rise in spending was mainly related to the sharp increase in petrol prices, with the average

price for 91-unleaded around the country climbing to a record high of over $3.60/ltr. That saw
spending on fuel rise 14% over the month.
• Those higher fuel prices, along with other cost of living pressures, have put the brakes on other
purchases. Spending in discretionary areas was down 0.4% in October and has essentially been
tracking sideways since May. There’s been notable softness in spending on travel and household
furnishings. However, spending in restaurants has been holding up.

Annual growth.
• While spending has certainly been constrained by the rise in living costs since the start of this year,
spending levels are still well above those we saw in 2025. And that’s not just because of high fuel
prices, with spending in discretionary areas also up compared to this time last year.
• That’s been supported by factors such as the drop in borrowing costs over the past year and
strength in export earnings. The boost from those factors is helping to balance the impacts of

factors such as softness in the labour market and high fuel prices. However, as we highlight over the
page, the strength in spending has been uneven across the country.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Regional breakdown
• As has been the case for some time, spending growth has been strongest in Southland. Other parts
of the country with strong rural backbones like Canterbury and Northland have also continued to
see solid gains.
• Spending growth remains more modest in Auckland. However, we expect that spending in Auckland

will take a step higher next month, with hospitality spending in particular likely to be boosted by
events like the Indian cricket tour, Diwali and Auckland Marathon.
• In a notable move, Wellington has shifted up the ranks and now sits in the middle of the pack.
Wellingtonians are no doubt holding their breath ahead of Saturday’s tight election and many will be
stocking up for the traditional election night barbecue and watch party.*

Document 419: Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

28 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
WHO FINANCES PRIVATE CREDIT IN THE UNITED STATES? WHAT THE FEDERAL RESERVE’S DATA REVEAL
Céline Choulet
SOURCE: FEDERAL RESERVE (ENHANCED FINANCIAL ACCOUNTS), BNP PARIBAS
The private credit market raises many questions, and its lack of transparency is not helping. Official public data remain scarce. The available
estimates come mainly from private sources, whose methodologies vary widely. The Financial Accounts of the Federal Reserve (Fed) now shed

new light on the matter. They reveal that private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt through households (31% of funds’ total financing),
insurers and pension funds (20%), and banks (19%).
Private credit can take several forms. The most common is loans granted by closed-end funds 1 to small and medium-sized, unlisted and highly
indebted companies. These loans are generally variable-rate, with flexible terms based on cash flow rather than a measure of profitability. While

this asset class has inherent vulnerabilities 2 (Bank of France, 2025), it is also particularly well-suited to financing innovative and long-term
projects (such as digital 3, infrastructure, renewable energy and healthcare), especially when combined with an equity stake in the company. Its
slower growth in Europe likely explains, in part, the region’s lag in innovation financing.
Private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt…
Private credit has grown rapidly in the United States over the past decade and has established itself as an alternative to bank loans for

a category of borrowers. It now accounts for 17.3% of the total outstanding loans to American non-financial corporations (NFCs) 4 and 7.1%
1 Strictly speaking, a closed-end fund has a fixed term during which investors cannot withdraw their invested capital. However, the sector has diversified since 2020. The Fed distinguishes
between 1) strictly closed-end private funds (private debt funds), 2) business development companies, which may have an indefinite lifespan and allow for occasional redemptions of

shares, and 3) interval and tender offer funds, which offer the possibility of scheduled redemptions.
2 Notably, high credit risk, high leverage, difficulty in valuing loans, opaque credit ratings, occasionally complex credit structures, high concentration within the sector and increasing
liquidity risk.
3 In 2025, nearly half of the direct loans made by private credit funds went to the tech sector (software, data centres and artificial intelligence) (Abbassi et al., 2026).

4 No comparable data exist for the Eurozone. According to France Invest (2026) data, private credit issued by French funds accounted for 4% of annual bank credit flows to NFCs in 2025.
Who finances US private credit funds?

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 28 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
of their debt. Approximately one-third (35%) is managed by strictly closed-end funds, with another third (36%) by semi-open-end funds
that allow occasional early redemptions. The remaining third is held on the balance sheets of non-resident private debt funds (25%) and
securitisation vehicles (4%).
… through banks, insurers and pension funds, and households

In yet another example of the lengthening of the credit intermediation chain in the United States (following that of the mortgage market),
banks are among the main creditors of private credit funds (65% of their debt and 19% of their total financing). The low risk weighting
of loans granted by banks to these vehicles, compared to that of direct bank loans to non-financial corporations, is undoubtedly a factor
(Chernenko et al., 2024). The holdings of insurers and pension funds – consisting mainly of shares in strictly closed-end funds – make

them among the leading investors in private debt funds (30% of issued shares and 20% of total financing). This asset class offers them the
opportunity to diversify their portfolios and attractive returns. The rise of semi-open-end funds has expanded the investor base to include
retail investors (31% of total financing), albeit at the cost of increased liquidity risk, as evidenced by the significant wave of redemptions in
late 2025 and early 2026 5. American households directly hold 70% of the equity issued by semi-open funds and 22% of the shares issued

by strictly closed funds 6.
Abbassi, P., Aldasoro, I., and Doerr, S. (2026), Financing the digital economy: the role of private credit, BIS Quarterly Review, September
2026
Banque de France (2025), The reshaping of the credit landscape in light of the development of private debt and Its implications for
financial stability, Financial Stability Report, December 2025
Chernenko, S., Ialenti, R. and Scharfstein, D. (2024), Bank capital and the growth of private credit, Working paper

France Invest (2026), Private debt fund activity in France, March 2026
celine.choulet@bnpparibas.com
5 Triggered by concerns regarding private credit funds’ high exposure to the software sector and the quality of the loans granted.
6 This exposure is likely overestimated because, in the Fed’s financial accounts, the household sector includes individuals and non-profit institutions, as well as resident private
equity funds, which form partnerships with private credit funds. In order to align more closely with the European definition, households here include sole proprietors.

重點摘要
3
• 五 中 全 會 公 報 隱 含 官 方
對 完 成 年 度 經 濟 目 標 具
相 當 信 心 ， 短 期 推 出 大
規 模 景 氣 刺 激 政 策 可 能
性降低。
• 五 中 全 會 前 後 顯 示 本 輪
政 治 整 頓 力 度 短 期 有 所
降溫， 但 整 體 水 準 仍 明
顯高於歷史均值。
《投資關鍵-反腐短暫趨緩，關注4Q景氣能否達標》
中國第三季實質GDP年增4.5%，前三季累計年增
4.6%，位居官方全年4.5%至5.0%目標區間下緣，
整體延續外強內弱格局。出口強勁，加上政策刺激
提供經濟底部支撐，然投資與消費兩大內需引擎同
步降溫，內生需求不足的結構性問題未有效改善。
展望後市，官方將擴大超長期特別國債與地方政府

專項債發行規模，加快重大基建項目落地，並持續
透過房地產去庫存、城中村改造等定向措施穩定內
需，搭配結構性寬鬆政策引導融資成本下行。惟五
中全會公報強調「確保完成全年經濟社會發展主要
目標任務」來看，官方對完成年度成長目標具相當
信心，因此第四季GDP年增率僅需維持約4.5%至
4.6%，短期推出大規模景氣刺激政策可能性降低。
本次五中全會主要聚焦黨的治理體系、監督機制與
自我革命等議題，並未釋出新一輪大規模財政或貨
幣刺激訊號。此外，觀察高盛編製的反腐敗代理指
標，五中全會前後雖略有回落，顯示本輪政治整頓
力度短期有所降溫，但整體水準仍明顯高於歷史均
值。隨著2027年新一屆黨領導層改組逐步接近，反
腐與監管力道仍有再度升溫的可能。在此背景下，
地方官員風險偏好與投資意願恐持續受到抑制，進
而削弱政策寬鬆措施向實體經濟的傳導效果，使中
國經濟仍面臨政策支持與內生需求不足並存的挑戰。
留意反貪腐力道若再度上升將壓抑政策傳導效果
資料來源：高盛，2026/10/30。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/2。

美國11/3將舉行參眾兩院的國會選舉，除視為對
當任總統的支持度檢驗之一，參眾兩院的黨派席次

回顧過去美股期中選舉期間，新興市場選前大多表
現壓抑，惟選後大多繳出正報酬佳績，上漲比率超
越五成。若以參眾兩院控制權皆翻轉的兩個年度
1994年、2006年來看，1944年因美聯準會啟動快
速升息循環，加上墨西哥金融危機爆發，導致選後
新興市場進入近一年的空頭走勢。然2006年即便
也是分裂政府，全球宏觀環境正受惠全球貿易繁榮、
商品超級周期推動經濟成長，新興市場選後12個

月累積報酬相當亮眼。

重點摘要
5
• 同樣面臨美國政治版圖翻
轉，新興市場指數表現卻
顯著差異，意味選後更取

• 過去美股期中選舉前。台
股的平均表現最為壓抑，
但選後反彈幅度亦最為顯
著，且正報酬機率維持在

比較1994年與2006年同樣面臨美國政治版圖翻轉，
指數表現卻顯著差異，意味選後更取決於景氣與獲
利週期，而非國會席次變化本身。
進一步檢視1990年以來美國期中選舉的各市場表
現，中國上證指數受自身政策與景氣循環影響較深，
與美國期中選舉關聯性相對有限外，多數市場皆呈
現選前承壓、選後回升的特徵。其中，台股於期中
選舉前的平均表現最為壓抑，但選後反彈幅度亦最
為顯著，且正報酬機率維持在較高水準。或反應美

國政治不確定性於選後下降，市場焦點重新回歸景
氣與企業獲利基本面，帶動資金自美國逐步向Beta
值較高的市場擴散。進一步而言，若相關國家具備
較穩健的經濟基本面、企業獲利動能或產業成長題
材支撐，則更有機會在風險偏好回升與資金重新配
置的過程中，成為資金優先流入的受惠對象
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。

重點摘要
6
• 美期中選對新興市場影響
較大的政策面向主要包括
關稅與貿易政策、債務上
限議題，以及政府對資料
中心建設與AI相關投資的
態度。
• 即使選後對資料中心的監
管聲浪升高，政策方向更
可能聚焦於強化治理框架
與技術管制，而非限制境
內AI投資或資料中心建設。
《投資關鍵-期中選舉後關注政策推動能力的變遷》
另一個觀察美國期中選舉後政黨版圖變化對新興市
場影響的角度，在於其對政策推動能力的改變。美
期中選對新興市場影響較大的政策面向主要包括關
稅與貿易政策、債務上限議題，以及政府對資料中
心建設與AI相關投資的態度。
首先，在關稅與貿易政策方面，由於關稅、地緣政
治及貿易談判等政策多屬行政權限範疇，政策方向
仍由白宮主導，且部分政策工具並不高度依賴國會

授權，因此政策波動性仍需關注。
其次，債務上限與財政融資需求則是影響資金流向
的重要觀察指標。國會僵局或限制積極的財政政策，
但也可能讓債務上限僵局上演，擾動市場風險偏好。
最後，近期美國各地對資料中心的反對聲浪升溫，
主要集中耗電、用水及對當地生活環境的影響。然
而，資料中心能否順利興建，關鍵仍掌握在州政府、
公共事業委員會及地方政府手中，因其負責電力、
水資源、土地分區與稅務優惠等審批事項。因此，
影響AI基建進度的主要變數未必是聯邦層級的期中
選舉，而更可能來自地方政府的政策取向。另一方
面，美國已將AI視為攸關國家競爭力、經濟成長與
國家安全的關鍵基礎設施，因此即使選後監管聲浪
升高，政策方向更可能聚焦於強化治理框架與技術
管制，而非限制境內AI投資或資料中心建設。換言
之，選後最大的差異較可能體現在建設進度與行政
程序，而非AI投資趨勢本身出現明顯逆轉。對以科
技硬體與AI供應鏈為主的新興亞洲市場而言，企業
獲利與資本支出動能仍將是比國會版圖變化更重要
的驅動因素。

重點摘要
7
• 期中選舉落幕後，焦點重
回美伊戰事若開打，油價
回升恐再度引發高利環境
的擔憂。
《投資關鍵-以長線投資視野聚焦穩健獲利標的》
展望後市，新興市場第四季仍將面臨波動升溫的環
境。 從歷史經驗與政策面觀察，美國期中選舉後
政治不確定性雖有望下降，但市場焦點最終仍將回
歸總體經濟、企業獲利與流動性環境。據傳美以軍

方擬定在美國期中選舉後再攻擊伊朗，而這可能再
度推升油價，進而引發市場對高利環境的擔憂。
基本面方面，市場預估新興市場經濟成長率將由
2026年第三季的3.4%升至第四季的4.4%，顯示整
體景氣仍具韌性。然而，高利率環境維持時間長於
預期，正考驗各國內需與投資動能；同時，已開發
國家央行偏鷹立場未見明顯鬆動，也使新興市場進
一步降息與推動寬鬆政策的空間受到壓縮。市場對
2027年新興市場景氣與企業獲利的預期差異恐將
擴大，資產表現也將更加分化。
台灣涵蓋晶圓代工、IC設計、先進封裝、AI伺服器、
PCB及網通設備等完整AI供應鏈。多層次且廣泛的
產業布局，不僅有助於分散單一產業景氣波動風險，
也使AI投資動能更全面傳導至企業獲利與出口表現，
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。

在「實體人工智慧」的第二支柱下，韓
國政府的目標是將韓國打造為全球人工
智慧機器人領域的前三名。重點領域包
括：1）製造業轉型為人工智慧；2）保
障核心人工智慧技術與資料安全；3）
建立區域性大規模生產中心。在另一份
新聞稿中，韓國政府指出，將投資 20
兆韓元用於人工智慧製造業，目標是創
造 100 兆韓元的附加價值。
重點摘要
8
• 台股或因美期中選舉落幕
後重啟反彈，但美伊戰事
的開打可能再度壓抑台股
表現，預計最壞情境可留
意年線支撐，但來年第一
季有望迎更穩健表現。
進而帶動台灣在主要新興市場中持續名列獲利預期
上修幅度領先族群。
隨著市場對AI投資效益與變現能力的檢驗日趨嚴格，
具備實質獲利支撐與產業擴散效益的市場，可望更

受資金青睞，而台股憑藉完整供應鏈優勢與穩健的
獲利上修趨勢，仍具相對吸引力。本行認為台股或
因美期中選舉落幕後重啟反彈，但美伊戰事的開打
可能再度壓抑台股表現，預計最壞情境可留意年線
支撐，但來年第一季有望迎更穩健表現。
韓國股市方面， 10 月 中 旬 受 長 鑫 存儲 宣 布 將 於
2026年底推出新一代DDR5 RDIMM伺服器記憶體
影響，拖累記憶體族群股價表現。本行認為，新產
品能否形成實質供給仍有賴良率提升及客戶採用進
度驗證。然而，此事件也凸顯韓股產業結構相對集
中於記憶體與半導體領域，使指數表現較容易受到
單一產業或個別企業消息影響。疊加市場期待韓國
政府推動的資本市場改革尚未取得實質進展，也導
致市場對過去低估的韓股評價重估行情有所失望，
除外資賣壓未歇外，韓股各投資類別資金流向，9
月較大股市資金流入來自三星與海力士的企業回購，
然企業回購已告一段落。預計韓股行情從單邊上漲
轉為區間震盪，建議投資建議暫為觀望。
資料來源：MS，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10。

在「實體人工智慧」的第二支柱下，韓

• 中國10月景氣數據轉弱，
短期政策重心可望回歸穩
增長，但全年目標達成有

• 外需韌性與內需疲弱並存，
聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼
國產替代政策扶植的科技
族群、稀缺原物料及高附
加出口價值等產業。惟短

回調影響，波動仍高，宜

搭配高股息金融股平衡投
資組合風險。
中國股市方面，儘管過去五中全會通常以審議下一
個五年規劃建議為主要任務，然由於四中全會已通
過十五五規劃，因此今年的五中全會聚焦國家安全
主軸並不意外。隨五中全會已遞補10位政治局委
員，高盛的反貪腐指數略下滑，短線政治整頓力度
降溫。中國10月官方製造業與服務業景氣領指標
再度低預期，同步跌破榮枯線，短線官方政策焦點
可能會重回穩增長，但由於達全年官方目標並不困
難，研判穩增長偏向支撐而非大力度刺激，政策傾
斜高端製造看來仍是主軸。
在整個中國經濟結構持續處外需韌性與內需疲弱的
拉鋸下，長線中國股市投資邏輯維持選股不選市，
聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼國產替代政策扶植的科技
族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出口價值等產業。但由
於短期中國科技股同步受海外科技股回調而整理，
建議嚴控持股比重，並可適時增持有高股息分紅防
禦的金融產業來平衡波動風險。

World Trends

2026 年 11 月 5 日

1/5 
ブラジル中銀は６会合ぶりに金利据え置き、ルラ再選による財政不安が影響 
～財政運営への信認回復が利下げ再開のカギに、高金利の長期化とレアル安への警戒続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ブラジル中央銀行は、11 月３～４日に開催した定例会合において、政策金利を 13.75％に据
え置くことを決定し、５会合連続で実施してきた利下げを一時休止した。背景には、大統領
選挙の決選投票で現職のルラ大統領が再選を果たしたことを受けて金融市場が混乱し、財政
運営への懸念からレアル安やインフレ期待の上昇が進んだことがある。 
○ 中銀は、足元で景気減速が鮮明になっているものの、インフレ率が目標を上回る状況が続い
ていることを警戒し、物価安定を優先する慎重姿勢を強めている。先行きの金融政策につい
ては、ルラ政権が財政健全化に向けた具体策を示し、市場の信認を回復できるかが重要とな
る。財政不安が後退し、レアル相場やインフレ期待が安定すれば利下げ再開の余地は広がる
ものの、財政拡張への懸念が払拭されなければ高金利政策が長期化する可能性がある。 
○ 高金利の長期化は景気減速や政府の利払い負担の増大を通じて財政悪化を招くことが懸念さ
れるため、財政政策と金融政策の整合性が一段と重要になる。当面のレアル相場は、財政運
営に対する市場の評価に左右される不安定な展開が続くと見込まれる。

【ブラジル中銀は６会合ぶりに政策金利を据え置き、利下げ局面の一時休止を決定】 
ブラジル中央銀行は、11 月３～４日の
日程で開催した定例の金融政策委員会
（COPOM）において、政策金利（Selic 金
利）を 13.75％に据え置くことを全会一
致で決定した（図１）。同行は、2026 年
３月の定例会合で約２年ぶりの利下げ
に舵を切り、その後は９月会合まで５会
合連続で 25bp ずつ政策金利を引き下げ
てきた。今回の決定は６会合ぶりの据え
置きとなり、３月会合以降、累計 125bp
に及ぶ利下げ局面を一時休止する形と
図 1 政策金利(Selic 金利)の推移

2/5 
なった。 
中銀が利下げを休止した背景には、10 月 25 日に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、現
職のルラ大統領が再選を果たしたことを受けて（注１）、同国金融市場が大きく混乱したことがあ
る。選挙前の金融市場においては、第１回投票でトップとなった右派のフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院
議員の勝利を織り込む動きが強まり、財政健全化への期待などを背景に、主要株価指数（ボベスパ
指数）や通貨レアル相場は堅調に推移してきた（注２）。しかし、ルラ氏が逆転勝利を収めたことを
受けて、政権の財政運営に対する不透明感があらためて意識されるとともに、株式、通貨、債券の
すべてに一転して売り圧力が強まった（注３）。その結果、通貨安に伴う輸入物価の押し上げやイン
フレ期待の上昇が懸念される事態となり、中銀は景気下支えに向けた金融緩和の継続から、物価安
定に向けた慎重な政策運営を優先する姿勢に転じたと考えられる。

【中銀はインフレ期待の一段の上昇を警戒、声明では政策運営の慎重姿勢を一段と強める】 
会合後に公表された声明文では、９月の前回会合以降、国内外の経済環境が一段と厳しさを増し
ているとの認識が示された。海外経済については、イラン情勢の長期化に伴う原油価格や石油製品
価格の上昇に加え、主要先進国における
金融引き締めの動きが、新興国経済を取
り巻く環境を悪化させていると指摘し
た。一方、国内経済については、景気の
減速が従来想定よりも鮮明になり、労働
市場にも頭打ちの兆しがみられるとし
た。ただし、物価については、総合イン
フレ率と基調的なインフレ率がともに
加速しており、中銀が掲げる目標レンジ
（３±1.5％）の上限を上回っているこ
とに警戒を示した（図２）。 
金融市場において、先行きのインフレ期待が一段と上昇していることにも注意が必要である。中
銀が金融機関などを対象に実施する市場予想調査（Focus）では、2026 年のインフレ率見通しは９
月会合時点の＋4.9％から＋5.2％へ、2027 年も＋4.3％から＋4.8％に上方修正されている。さら
に、中銀自身が示した金融政策の効果を評価する対象期間である2028 年４-６月時点のインフレ率
見通しも＋3.5％と、目標を上回る水準にとどまっている。このため、インフレ率が上振れするリス

注１ 10 月26 日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666866.html） 
注２ 10 月６日付レポート「金融市場はブラジルの右派回帰を好感、株価・レアルともに上昇」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666333.html） 
注３ 10 月27 日付レポート「ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666918.html）

3/5 
クが下振れリスクを上回っているとしたうえで、通貨安の長期化による輸入物価への波及や、原油
価格上昇の二次的な影響、財政刺激策による需要の押し上げなどを警戒する姿勢を示した。 
先行きの政策運営については、具体的な利下げ再開時期を示すことはせず、物価目標への収束に
必要な金融引き締めの程度を維持する方針を強調した。９月会合まで記されていた金融緩和の継続
を示唆する文言は削除されるなど緩和姿勢は後退しており、金融緩和の継続を前提としない姿勢に
変化している様子がうかがえる。しかし、中銀は現行の金利水準について、十分に引き締め的と評
価しており、景気減速の強まりも認識している。このため、今回の判断は直ちに金融引き締めへの
転換を示すものではなく、インフレ期待や金融市場の動向を見極めるための予防的な利下げ休止と
考えられる。

【金融市場は、ルラ政権の財政運営がインフレ期待や金利の上昇圧力を強める可能性を警戒】 
前述のように、同国金融市場においては、大統領選挙の決選投票の結果を受けて、翌 26 日にブラ
ジル資産を売却する動きが急速に広が
った。その後は、主要株価指数、レアル
相場ともに一定の持ち直しの動きをみ
せたものの、不安定な動きが続いている
（図３）。こうした動きは、金融市場の混
乱が一時的なポジション調整による側
面を有する一方、ブラジルの財政運営に
対する投資家の警戒感が払拭されてい
ないことを示唆している。こうしたな
か、今後の金融市場の動向を左右する重
要な焦点となるのが、ルラ政権の財政運
営である。ルラ政権は、低所得者層への所得再分配や社会保障の拡充、公共投資を重視する政策を
掲げてきた。決選投票の前には、低所得者向け現金給付制度（ボルサ・ファミリア）の給付額を 15％
引き上げる方針などを打ち出した。ルラ政権は、既存予算の組み替えなどによって財政ルールや基
礎的財政収支の目標を維持する方針を示しているものの、社会保障支出や公共投資の拡大と財政健
全化を両立できるかについて、金融市場では慎重な見方が強まっている。

### Page 4

4/5 
ブラジル政府が８月末に提出した2027 年予算案では、基礎的財政収支についてＧＤＰ比0.5％の
黒字目標を掲げ、財政ルールに基づく調整後の黒字額を 834 億レアルと見込んでいる。しかし、調
整前の黒字額は 186 億レアルにとどま
る。一方で、10 月時点の民間予測によれ
ば、2027 年の中央政府の基礎的財政収支
は440 億レアル程度の赤字となる見通し
となっている。さらに、同調査では、一
般政府の債務残高が 2026 年のＧＤＰ比
83.4％から 2027 年には 87.0％へ上昇す
ると見込まれており（図４）、財政の持続
可能性を巡る懸念は根強く残る。こうし
たなか、財政政策に対する信認が十分に
回復しなければ、国債利回りの高止まり
やレアル安を通じて金融環境の不安定化が続くことが予想される。 
とはいえ、ルラ政権が財政規律を放棄する方針を示していると判断するのは早計である。ドゥリ
ガン財務相は、選挙後の金融市場の混乱について、それまで積み上がっていたポジションの巻き戻
しによる影響が大きいとの認識を示すとともに、政府として財政規律を維持する方針をあらためて
強調している。このため、今後の金融市場の動向は、財政支出の規模だけでなく、歳出抑制策や増
収策の具体化、2027 年予算案の実現可能性、経済政策を担う閣僚の人選などによって左右されると
考えられる。 
中銀も、声明のなかで、国内の財政政策が金融政策や金融資産の価格形成に及ぼす影響を引き続
き注視する方針を明示している。財政拡張による国内需要の押し上げに加え、財政不安を背景とす
るレアル安やインフレ期待の上昇が続けば、物価の安定に必要な金利水準が高止まりする可能性が
ある。さらに、高金利の長期化は政府の利払い負担を増大させ、財政状況を一段と悪化させる要因
となり得る。このため、今後のブラジル経済を巡っては、財政政策と金融政策の整合性を確保でき
るかが重要な課題になると考えられる。

【先行きの政策運営は財政政策への信認回復がカギを握る一方、レアル相場には不透明感が残る】 
先行きの金融政策については、今回の利下げ休止を受けて、高金利政策が当初想定よりも長期化
する可能性が高まっている。大統領選挙後に公表された Focus 調査では、2026 年末の政策金利見通
しが従来の 13.25％から 13.50％に、2027 年末についても 11.75％から 12.50％へと引き上げられ
た。これは、金融市場においては、中銀による段階的な利下げ再開を想定しているものの、そのペ
ースは従来に比べて緩やかになると見込んでいることを示している。 
今後の政策運営を巡っては、ルラ政権が財政健全化に向けた具体策を示すことにより、金融市場
の信認を回復できるかが重要となる。財政運営への懸念が後退し、レアル相場が安定、インフレ期
待の低下が確認されれば、中銀が段階的な利下げを再開する余地は広がると考えられる。一方で、

### Page 5

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
5/5
財政拡張に対する懸念が払拭されず、レアル安やインフレ期待の上昇が続く場合には、金融緩和の
休止が一段と長期化する可能性がある。さらに、原油価格の高止まりなど外部環境の悪化が重なれ
ば、追加的な金融引き締めを迫られる展開も否定できない。
一方で、足元のブラジル経済は、これまでの高金利政策の影響で内需を中心とする景気減速の動
きが鮮明になりつつある。高金利の長期化は、家計消費や企業の設備投資を抑制するだけでなく、
政府の資金調達コストを押し上げることを通じて財政状況にも影響を与える。このため、財政運営
に対する信認の回復が遅れる場合、中銀は景気下支えに向けた金融緩和と、インフレ抑制に向けた
金融引き締めの間で難しい判断を迫られることになる。

当面のレアル相場は、ルラ政権の財政政策に対する市場の評価に左右されやすい展開が続くと考
えられる。財政健全化に向けた実効性のある政策が示されれば、国債利回りの低下や海外資金の流
入を通じてレアル相場が持ち直す余地はある。しかし、具体的な方向性が示されない場合には、財
政リスクを警戒した資金流出が続く可能性がある。中銀にとっては、財政政策に起因する金融市場
の不安定化が物価安定の妨げとなることをいかに回避するかが、今後の政策運営における重要な課
題になると見込まれる。
以 上

Status: ok

### Page 1

（備考）2025年度までの実績は内閣府「国民経済計算」により作成。

１. 世界の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は、2022 年以降３％台半ばで推移しているが、少子高齢化を背
景とした新興国の成長鈍化により、予測期間の終期の 2036 年には２％台後半まで低下す
ると予想する。 
２. 日本の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は、2036 年度までの先行き 10 年間の平均で 0.9％と予想する。
潜在成長率は足下のゼロ％台後半から 2020 年代後半に１％程度まで高まった後、少子高
齢化の更なる進展に伴い 2030年代前半にゼロ％台後半まで鈍化すると見込む。 
３. 消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品を除く総合）は、先行き 10 年間の平均で 2.1％と予想する。
企業等の各経済主体の期待物価上昇率は２％前後となってきている。中東情勢の悪化に
よる物価上昇が落ち着くにつれ、2028 年度以降は、飲食料品の消費税率の引下げ・引上
げの影響を除けば２％程度の安定的な物価上昇が実現すると見込む。 
４. 日本銀行は、当面は物価上昇が再燃する中、消費者物価上昇率を２％程度に安定させてい
くため、段階的に利上げを実施し、政策金利は 2027 年度に1.75％まで引き上げられると
見込む。長期金利は、長期国債の買い入れ減額の継続に加え、国内の貯蓄超過の縮小が上
昇圧力になることから、2036 年度には３％台後半まで上昇すると予想する。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-13

中期経済見通し（2026～2036 年度）

経済研究部

### Page 2

主要先進国の成長率はコロナ禍で大きく落ち込んだものの、その後は相次ぐショックにもかかわ
らず底堅く推移している。ホルムズ海峡の輸送が制約される中では、世界的な原油在庫の取り崩し、
エネルギー需要抑制や政府主導での節約措置、供給経路・供給源の多様化（中東産原油の代替ルー
トでの輸送や中東以外の原油増産）などが供給制約ショックを吸収している。 
インフレ率は 2022 年のエネルギー価格高騰などを背景に急上昇したのち、2025 年にかけて緩や
かに低下してきた。しかし、コロナ禍前の水準まで低下しないまま、中東情勢の悪化で再び上昇圧
力が強まっている。2022年のインフレ加速期に、主要中央銀行による金融引き締めが遅れた経験も
あり、中央銀行はインフレ警戒感を強めており、利上げに転じる国も増えつつある。

（ＡＩ関連需要が成長率を押し上げ） 
成長率の底堅さの要因として、足もとで顕著となっているのが世界的なＡＩ関連需要の高まりで
ある。半導体など電子部品需要、データセンターを中心とした建設需要、電力などのインフラ投資
需要が増加している。世界半導体売上高（3か月移動平均）は急増しており、2026 年5 月以降、前
年比で 2倍を超えている。米中を中心にＡＩ開発が急速に進んでいるほか、ＡＩ関連需要は貿易を
通じて特にアジアの生産・輸出を押し上げるなど、世界に波及している。

### Page 3

こうした状況下、主要先進国では長期金利の上昇圧力が強まっている。インフレ圧力の強まりと
中央銀行の利上げ観測のほか、政府の財政支出拡大懸念や民間部門での資金調達需要が金利の押し
上げ圧力となっている。 
財政支出に関して、米国では主要財源と見られた関税引き上げ政策のうち、最高裁判所により国
際緊急経済権限法（ＩＥＥＰＡ）に基づく関税が無効と判断され、歳入の不透明感が増した。欧州
ではトランプ政権による安全保障政策の方針転換を背景に防衛費を拡大する動きが進む。日本では、
ガソリン価格抑制策など物価高対策が長期化するなか、「責任ある積極財政」を掲げる高市政権の
財政支出に注目が集まっている。民間部門の資金調達需要の高まりは、巨大ＩＴ企業によるＡＩ関
連投資の拡大などが背景にある。

（経済を取り巻く不確実性は引き続き高い） 
世界経済を取り巻く不確実性は高い状態が続いている。トランプ大統領の講じる政策は予見可能
性が低い。今後も通商領域やその他の領域で国家間の摩擦が強まる可能性がある。短期的な経済シ
ョックが生じうるだけでなく、中長期的にはトランプ大統領の米国第一主義や既存の国際秩序や多
国間枠組みを見直す政策姿勢がドル資産への需要やドルの基軸通貨としての地位に影響を及ぼす
可能性もあるだろう。地政学的リスクも大きく、すでにウクライナや中東地域では地政学的要因が
経済に悪影響を及ぼしている。原油在庫の取り崩しや供給源の多様化といったバッファーには量的

### Page 4

（世界の実質経済成長率は緩やかに低下） 
世界経済は、新型コロナウイルス感染症の影響で 2020 年に▲2.7％の大幅なマイナス成長となっ
た後、2021年はその反動で 6.7％の高成長となった。その後は、ロシアによるウクライナ侵攻、米
国による関税率の大幅な引き上げ、中東情勢の悪化など、相次ぐショックに見舞われてきた。しか
し、世界経済はこうした逆風のなかでも底堅さを保ち、実質経済成長率は 2022年以降、3％台半ば
で推移している。中期的には予測期間にわたって緩やかな鈍化傾向をたどり、予測期間末の2036 年
には 2％台後半まで低下することが見込まれる。 
先行きの成長率を先進国と新興国に分けてみると、新興国の成長率は先進国を一貫して上回ると
みられる。しかし、少子高齢化に伴い潜在成長率の低下が進むことなどを背景に、新興国の成長率
寄与度は予測期間後半に 2％台前半まで低下すると予想する。

世界経済に占める新興国の割合（ドルベース）は、2000 年の 20％程度から足もとでは 40％程度
まで上昇している。新興国の成長率は今後緩やかに低下するものの、先進国を上回る成長が続くこ
とから、その割合は予測期間末には 40％台半ばまで高まるだろう。

### Page 5

国別にみると、世界第 2 位の経済規模を持つ中国は、2018 年に経済規模でユーロ圏を上回るな
ど、コロナ禍前まで世界経済に占める割合を拡大させてきた。しかし、先行きについては、中国の
名目成長率が予測期間後半にかけて緩やかに鈍化し、人民元の対ドル相場が横ばい圏にとどまるこ
とから、予測期間中は米国の経済規模が中国を一貫して上回ると見込まれる。 
中国に匹敵する人口を抱えるインドは、予測期間中は人口増加が続くことから高い潜在成長率を
期待でき、世界経済に占める割合を高めていくとみられる。2020 年代後半にはインドの経済規模が
日本を上回ることが予想される。 
一人当たりＧＤＰ（ドルベース）をみると、日本は 1980年代後半から 1990 年代にかけて米国を
上回っていたが、2000 年頃にその関係が逆転した後は一貫して米国を下回っている。日本の一人当
たりＧＤＰは円安の影響もあり、2025 年には米国の 4 割程度の水準まで低下した。今後 10 年間の
日本の平均成長率は米国を下回ることが予想されるため、予測期間終盤も日本は米国の 4割弱の水
準で推移することとなるだろう。また、ユーロ圏と比較すると、2025年時点で日本の一人当たりＧ
ＤＰはおよそ 7 割であるが、予測期間の平均成長率がユーロ圏を下回るため、予測期間後半には 6
割程度まで低下するだろう。 
一方、日本のＧＤＰの水準は 2010年に中国に抜かれたが、一人当たりＧＤＰでみれば 2025 年時
点でも中国の2.5 倍程度の水準にある。今後は中国の成長率が日本を大きく上回るが、予測期間末
でも日本の一人当たりＧＤＰは中国の 2倍程度の水準を維持するだろう。また、予測期間中盤に日
本のＧＤＰを抜くインドは、一人当たりＧＤＰでみれば 2025 年時点では日本の約 7％となってい
るが、10年後には 11％程度の水準まで緩やかに上昇するだろう。
